  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tláloc adalah dewa hujan dalam agama Aztec, juga dewa kesuburan tanah dan air, yang dipuja sebagai pemberi kehidupan sekaligus ditakuti karena kuasanya atas hujan es, guntur, dan kilat.",
    "en": "Tláloc is the god of rain in Aztec religion, also a deity of earthly fertility and water, worshipped as a giver of life and feared for his power over hail, thunder and lightning.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tlaloc-c01",
      "tlaloc-c02",
      "tlaloc-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tláloc adalah dewa hujan dalam agama Aztec dan juga dewa kesuburan tanah serta air. Ia dipuja sebagai pemberi kehidupan dan rezeki, dan banyak ritual serta korban dipersembahkan atas namanya. Ia ditakuti karena kuasanya atas hujan es, guntur, kilat, dan hujan, meskipun bukan sosok yang jahat.",
      "en": "Tláloc is the god of rain in Aztec religion and also a deity of earthly fertility and water. He was worshipped as a giver of life and sustenance, and many rituals and sacrifices were held in his name. He was feared for his power over hail, thunder, lightning and rain, although not as a malicious figure.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tlaloc-c01",
        "tlaloc-c02",
        "tlaloc-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sumber lain menggambarkannya sebagai dewa yang berwajah ganda: murah hati ketika memberi hujan, tetapi tak kenal ampun ketika mengirim badai dan kekeringan. Dalam gambaran tentang kendi air, ia menurunkan hujan dari kendi di timur, sedangkan dari kendi lain ia menurunkan kekeringan, penyakit, dan embun beku. Hujan juga dipercaya turun ketika para Tláloc memukul pecah kendi di dalam gunung, dan guntur dianggap bunyi pecahnya kendi itu.",
      "en": "Another source describes him as two-sided: benevolent when giving rain but unforgiving when sending storms and drought. In the imagery of the water jars, he gave out rain from the jar of the East and drought, disease and frost from the other jars. Rain was also thought to come when the Tlálocs smashed the jars inside mountains, and thunder was taken to be the noise of the jars breaking.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tlaloc-c06",
        "tlaloc-c08",
        "tlaloc-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tláloc dikaitkan dengan gua, mata air, dan gunung, terutama gunung suci tempat ia dipercaya bersemayam. Ia juga dianggap penguasa para Tlaloque dan surga Tlalocán, tempat korban banjir, badai, dan penyakit diterima setelah mati. Untuk menenangkannya dipersembahkan korban, termasuk anak-anak yang tangisnya dianggap pertanda baik. Di Tenochtitlán, ia memiliki kuil kembar di Templo Mayor yang kedudukannya setara dengan kuil Huitzilopochtli.",
      "en": "Caves, springs and mountains are linked with Tláloc, above all the sacred mountain believed to be his home. He was also considered ruler of the Tlaloque and of the paradise of Tlalocán, the destination after death of those claimed by floods, storms and disease. To appease him, offerings were made, including child victims whose tears were read as a good sign. At Tenochtitlán he had a twin temple on the Templo Mayor, equal in status to that of Huitzilopochtli.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tlaloc-c04",
        "tlaloc-c10",
        "tlaloc-c13",
        "tlaloc-c12",
        "tlaloc-c11"

    {
      "id": "Dalam seni, Tláloc lazim digambarkan bersama ular, dengan mata bulat besar dan taring besar; sumber lain menyebut taring dan mata bercincin sebagai penandanya. Penggambaran tertuanya berasal dari vas abad ke-1 SM, dan di beberapa budaya Mesoamerika ia muncul dengan nama lain seperti Cocijo bagi Zapotec.",
      "en": "In art, Tláloc is commonly depicted with snakes, goggled eyes and large fangs; another source names fangs and ringed eyes as his identifiers. His earliest depictions are on 1st-century BCE vases, and in several Mesoamerican cultures he appears under other names, such as Cocijo for the Zapotec.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c01",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s1",
      "quote": "is the god of rain in Aztec religion.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tláloc adalah dewa hujan dalam agama Aztec.",
        "en": "Tláloc is the god of rain in Aztec religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c02",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s1",
      "quote": "He was also a deity of earthly fertility and water, and worshipped as a giver of life and sustenance; many rituals and sacrifices predicated upon these aspects were held in his name.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah dewa kesuburan tanah dan air yang dipuja sebagai pemberi kehidupan.",
        "en": "He was also a deity of earthly fertility and water, worshipped as a giver of life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c03",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s1",
      "quote": "He was feared—albeit not as a malicious figure—for his power over hail, thunder, lightning, and rain.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia ditakuti karena kekuasaannya atas hujan es, guntur, dan kilat.",
        "en": "He was feared for his power over hail, thunder, and lightning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c04",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s1",
      "quote": "He is also associated with caves, springs, and mountains, most specifically the sacred mountain where he was believed to reside.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diasosiasikan dengan gua, mata air, dan puncak gunung tinggi.",
        "en": "He is also associated with caves, springs, and mountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c05",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "is one of the most important and formidable gods in the Aztec pantheon. He was considered the god of rain, water, lightning and agriculture.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tláloc adalah salah satu dewa terpenting dan paling menakutkan dalam jajaran dewa Aztec, dianggap dewa hujan, air, petir, dan pertanian.",
        "en": "Tláloc is one of the most important and formidable gods of the Aztec pantheon, considered the god of rain, water, lightning and agriculture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c06",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "Tlaloc was seen as both a benevolent god providing life-giving rain but also as an unforgiving and destructive deity when he sent storms and drought.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tlaloc dipandang sebagai dewa yang murah hati karena memberi hujan penghidupan, tetapi juga sebagai dewa yang tak kenal ampun dan merusak ketika mengirim badai dan kekeringan.",
        "en": "Tlaloc was seen both as a benevolent god providing life-giving rain and as an unforgiving, destructive deity when he sent storms and drought."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c07",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "The name Tláloc derives from the Nahuatl words tlali meaning 'earth' and oc meaning 'something on the surface'.",
      "locator": "Name & Manifestations",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Tláloc berasal dari kata Nahuatl tlali yang berarti 'bumi' dan oc yang berarti 'sesuatu di permukaan'.",
        "en": "The name Tláloc derives from the Nahuatl words tlali, meaning 'earth', and oc, meaning 'something on the surface'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c08",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "From the jar of the East Tláloc gave out the rains so essential for life whilst from the other jars the god dispensed the terrible scourges of drought, disease and frost so deadly for mankind and crops.",
      "locator": "Name & Manifestations",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari kendi di arah timur, Tláloc mengirim hujan yang penting bagi kehidupan, sedangkan dari kendi-kendi lain ia menurunkan kekeringan, penyakit, dan embun beku yang mematikan bagi manusia dan tanaman.",
        "en": "From the jar of the East Tláloc gave out the rains essential for life, whereas from the other jars he dispensed drought, disease and frost, deadly for mankind and crops."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c09",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "Alternatively, rain was thought to come when the Tlálocs used sticks to smash the water jars which were inside mountains. Indeed, the sound of thunder was believed to be the noise made when these water jars were smashed.",
      "locator": "Name & Manifestations",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hujan juga dipercaya turun ketika para Tláloc memukul kendi air di dalam gunung dengan tongkat, dan suara guntur dipercaya sebagai bunyi pecahnya kendi-kendi itu.",
        "en": "Rain was also thought to come when the Tlálocs used sticks to smash the water jars inside mountains, and the sound of thunder was believed to be the noise of these jars being smashed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c10",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "Tláloc was also considered the ruler of the Tlaloque - a motley group of rain, weather and mountain gods",
      "locator": "Name & Manifestations",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tláloc juga dianggap penguasa para Tlaloque, sekelompok dewa hujan, cuaca, dan gunung yang beragam.",
        "en": "Tláloc was also considered the ruler of the Tlaloque, a motley group of rain, weather and mountain gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c11",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "At the Aztec capital of Tenochtitlán two twin temples were set up on the Templo Mayor pyramid, one dedicated to the great god Huitzilopochtli (representing the dry season) and the other to Tláloc who was given equal status.",
      "locator": "Worship & Ritual",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di ibu kota Aztec, Tenochtitlán, dua kuil kembar didirikan di piramida Templo Mayor: satu untuk Huitzilopochtli (musim kemarau) dan satu untuk Tláloc, yang diberi kedudukan setara.",
        "en": "At the Aztec capital Tenochtitlán, two twin temples were set up on the Templo Mayor pyramid, one for Huitzilopochtli (the dry season) and one for Tláloc, who was given equal status."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c12",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "More sinister offerings to appease the god and win his favour were sacrificial victims, including children, whose tears were seen as a favourable sign and linked to drops of rain from Tláloc himself.",
      "locator": "Worship & Ritual",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk menenangkan dewa ini dipersembahkan korban, termasuk anak-anak, yang tangisnya dianggap pertanda baik dan dikaitkan dengan tetes hujan dari Tláloc sendiri.",
        "en": "To appease the god, sacrificial victims were offered, including children, whose tears were seen as a favourable sign and linked to drops of rain from Tláloc himself."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c13",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "Tláloc was also believed to rule the other-worldly paradise of Tlalocán where victims of floods, storms and diseases such as leprosy were received after death.",
      "locator": "Worship & Ritual",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tláloc dipercaya menguasai surga Tlalocán, tempat korban banjir, badai, dan penyakit seperti lepra diterima setelah mati.",
        "en": "Tláloc was believed to rule the other-worldly paradise of Tlalocán, where victims of floods, storms and diseases such as leprosy were received after death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c14",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "In later Postclassical Mexico Tláloc was believed to live in caves which were suitably damp for a rain god but also filled with magnificent treasures.",
      "locator": "Worship & Ritual",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada masa Pascaklasik di Meksiko, Tláloc dipercaya tinggal di gua-gua yang lembap dan penuh harta indah.",
        "en": "In later Postclassical Mexico, Tláloc was believed to live in caves that were damp and filled with magnificent treasures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c15",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "The earliest representations of Tláloc in art belong to 1st century BCE vases from Tlapacoya where the god wields a lightning bolt.",
      "locator": "Representations in Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran tertua Tláloc dalam seni berasal dari vas abad ke-1 SM di Tlapacoya, tempat sang dewa memegang petir.",
        "en": "The earliest representations of Tláloc in art belong to 1st-century BCE vases from Tlapacoya, where the god wields a lightning bolt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c16",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "Tláloc is commonly depicted in the company of snakes. He most often has goggled eyes and large fangs like a jaguar",
      "locator": "Representations in Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tláloc lazim digambarkan bersama ular; ia paling sering bermata bulat besar seperti kacamata renang dan bertaring besar seperti jaguar.",
        "en": "Tláloc is commonly depicted in the company of snakes, most often with goggled eyes and large fangs like a jaguar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c17",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "In the various Mesoamerican cultures Tláloc appears as Dzahui for the Mixtec, Tajίn for the Totonac, Chupithripeme for the Tarascan and Cocijo for the Zapotec.",
      "locator": "Name & Manifestations",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam berbagai budaya Mesoamerika, Tláloc muncul sebagai Dzahui bagi suku Mixtec, Tajín bagi Totonac, Chupithripeme bagi Tarasca, dan Cocijo bagi Zapotec.",
        "en": "In the various Mesoamerican cultures Tláloc appears as Dzahui for the Mixtec, Tajín for the Totonac, Chupithripeme for the Tarascan and Cocijo for the Zapotec."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c18",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "Tlaloc was important to the Aztecs because he was the god of vital rains needed for agriculture.",
      "locator": "Questions & Answers",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tlaloc penting bagi bangsa Aztec karena ia dewa hujan vital yang dibutuhkan untuk pertanian.",
        "en": "Tlaloc was important to the Aztecs because he was the god of vital rains needed for agriculture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c19",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s1",
      "quote": "Tláloc's two main identifiers are fangs, along with ringed eyes.",
      "locator": "Deity iconography",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dua penanda utama Tláloc adalah taring dan mata bercincin.",
        "en": "Tláloc's two main identifiers are fangs and ringed eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c20",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "In the Aztec Creation myth Tláloc was ruler of the 3rd Sun, he was linked to Mázatl (Deer) the 7th day",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitos penciptaan Aztec, Tláloc adalah penguasa Matahari ke-3 dan dikaitkan dengan Mázatl (Rusa), hari ketujuh.",
        "en": "In the Aztec creation myth, Tláloc was ruler of the 3rd Sun and was linked to Mázatl (Deer), the 7th day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c21",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "Born during the Creation when Quetzalcoatl and Huitzilpochtli (or in some versions Tezcatlipoca) dismembered the reptilian monster Cipactli",
      "locator": "Name & Manifestations",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tláloc lahir pada masa penciptaan, ketika Quetzalcoatl dan Huitzilpochtli (atau dalam beberapa versi Tezcatlipoca) mencabik-cabik monster reptil Cipactli.",
        "en": "Tláloc was born during the Creation, when Quetzalcoatl and Huitzilpochtli (or in some versions Tezcatlipoca) dismembered the reptilian monster Cipactli."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tlaloc-c22",
      "source_id": "tlaloc-s2",
      "quote": "along with his sister Chalchiúhtlicue (or in some versions his wife or mother), herself a goddess of rivers, oceans and floods.",
      "locator": "Name & Manifestations",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chalchiúhtlicue, dewi sungai, samudra, dan banjir, disebut sebagai saudari Tláloc atau, dalam beberapa versi, istri atau ibunya.",
        "en": "Chalchiúhtlicue, a goddess of rivers, oceans and floods, is called the sister of Tláloc or, in some versions, his wife or mother."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Siapa yang mencabik-cabik Cipactli saat Tláloc lahir",
        "en": "Who dismembered Cipactli at Tláloc's birth"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Quetzalcoatl dan Huitzilpochtli.",
            "en": "Quetzalcoatl and Huitzilpochtli."

            "tlaloc-c21"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Dalam beberapa versi, Quetzalcoatl dan Tezcatlipoca.",
            "en": "In some versions, Quetzalcoatl and Tezcatlipoca."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Huitzilopochtli adalah dewa matahari dan perang dalam agama Aztec serta dewa pelindung bangsa Aztec dan ibu kota mereka, Tenochtitlán.",
    "en": "Huitzilopochtli is the solar and war deity in Aztec religion and the patron god of the Aztecs and their capital, Tenochtitlán.",

    {
      "id": "Huitzilopochtli adalah salah satu dewa terpenting dalam jajaran dewa Aztec, dan bagi bangsa Méxica ia dewa tertinggi. Ia dikenal sebagai dewa matahari dan perang, pelindung Tenochtitlán, dan dikaitkan dengan emas, prajurit, serta penguasa. Ia menggunakan Xiuhcoatl, ular api, sebagai senjata, sehingga dikaitkan juga dengan api. Menurut sumber lain, ia semula kurang penting bagi bangsa Nahua sampai Tlacaelel mereformasi agama Aztec dan menempatkannya sejajar dengan Quetzalcoatl, Tlaloc, dan Tezcatlipoca.",
      "en": "Huitzilopochtli ranks among the foremost deities of the Aztec pantheon, and the Méxica held him to be the highest god. He is the god of sun and war, protector of Tenochtitlán, linked with gold, warriors and rulers. His weapon was Xiuhcoatl, the fire serpent, which ties him to fire as well. Another source adds that he was of little importance to the Nahuas until Tlacaelel reformed Aztec religion and raised him to the level of Quetzalcoatl, Tlaloc and Tezcatlipoca.",
      "claim_ids": [

    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah yang terkenal, Huitzilopochtli membalas pembunuhan ibunya, Coatlícue, dengan mengalahkan saudara-saudaranya di Gunung Coatepec. Ia melompat keluar dari jasad ibunya dengan senjata lengkap dan memenggal Coyolxauhqui, yang kepalanya kemudian menjadi bulan. Bagi bangsa Aztec, pergulatan itu terulang setiap hari dan menjelaskan pergantian antara matahari dan bulan di langit.",
      "en": "In a famous story, Huitzilopochtli avenged the murder of his mother Coatlícue by defeating his siblings on Mt. Coatepec.       "en": "The Méxica believed Huitzilopochtli led them from Aztlán to Tenochtitlán. In that capital he had a wooden statue and shrine atop the Temple Mayor pyramid, alongside that of Tláloc.       "type": "temple",
      "description": {
        "id": "Di puncak piramida ini Huitzilopochtli memiliki patung kayu dan kuil di samping milik Tláloc.",
        "en": "Atop this pyramid Huitzilopochtli had a wooden statue and shrine alongside that of Tláloc."
      },
      "claim_ids": [
        "huitzilopochtli-c16"
      ]
    },
    {
      "name": {
        "id": "Gunung Coatepec",

      },
      "type": "mountain",
      "description": {
        "id": "Gunung ular, tempat Huitzilopochtli mengalahkan saudara-saudaranya.",
        "en": "The snake mountain, where Huitzilopochtli defeated his siblings."
      },
      "claim_ids": [

      ]
    },
    {
      "name": {
        "id": "Danau Texcoco",
        "en": "Lake Texcoco"
      },
      "type": "lake",
      "description": {
        "id": "Tempat jantung pemberontak dilempar atas perintah sang dewa untuk menandai lokasi rumah baru bangsa Méxica.",
        "en": "Where the rebel's heart was thrown at the god's instruction to mark the site of the Méxica's new home."
      },

        "en": "His mother in the Coatepec story, whose murder he avenged or whom (in some versions) he saved; in an alternative version he is the offspring of Coatlícue, the supreme earth goddess."

  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [
    {
      "id": "Mengapa kisah kelahiran di Coatepec dianggap menjelaskan pergantian matahari dan bulan?",
      "en": "Why was the Coatepec birth story taken to explain the exchange between sun and moon?"
    },
    {
      "id": "Bagaimana persembahan kepada Huitzilopochtli mencerminkan pandangan bangsa Aztec tentang kelangsungan matahari?",
      "en": "How do the offerings to Huitzilopochtli reflect the Aztec view of the Sun's survival?"
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",

      "id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",

      "title": "Huitzilopochtli",
      "author": "Mark Cartwright",
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": "2013-08-27",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-06"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c01",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "He was also the patron god of the Aztecs and their capital city, Tenochtitlan.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah dewa pelindung bangsa Aztecs dan ibu kota Tenochtitlan.",
        "en": "He was also the patron god of the Aztecs and their capital city, Tenochtitlan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c02",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "He wielded Xiuhcoatl, the fire serpent, as a weapon, thus also associating Huitzilopochtli with fire.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menggunakan Xiuhcoatl sang ular api sebagai senjatanya.",
        "en": "He wielded Xiuhcoatl, the fire serpent, as a weapon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c03",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "The Spaniards recorded the deity's name as Huichilobos.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Spanyol mencatat nama dewa ini sebagai Huichilobos.",
        "en": "The Spaniards recorded the deity's name as Huichilobos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c05",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "was one of the most important deities in the Aztec pantheon and for the Méxica he was the supreme god.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huitzilopochtli adalah salah satu dewa terpenting dalam jajaran dewa Aztec, dan bagi bangsa Méxica ia dewa tertinggi.",
        "en": "Huitzilopochtli was one of the most important deities in the Aztec pantheon, and for the Méxica he was the supreme god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c06",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "He was the god of the sun and war, considered the patron of the Aztec capital Tenochtitlán and associated with gold, warriors and rulers.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dewa matahari dan perang, dianggap pelindung ibu kota Aztec Tenochtitlán, serta dikaitkan dengan emas, prajurit, dan penguasa.",
        "en": "He was the god of the sun and war, considered the patron of the Aztec capital Tenochtitlán and associated with gold, warriors and rulers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c07",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "Generally it is agreed that there are two elements, huītzilin \"hummingbird\" and ōpōchtli \"left hand side\".",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Umumnya disepakati bahwa namanya terdiri atas dua unsur: huītzilin yang berarti burung kolibri dan ōpōchtli yang berarti sisi tangan kiri.",
        "en": "It is generally agreed that his name has two elements: huītzilin, \"hummingbird\", and ōpōchtli, \"left hand side\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c08",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "The name is often translated as \"Left-Handed Hummingbird\" or \"Hummingbird of the South\" on the basis that Aztec cosmology associated the south with the left hand side of the body.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama itu sering diterjemahkan sebagai \"Kolibri Kidal\" atau \"Kolibri dari Selatan\", karena kosmologi Aztec mengaitkan selatan dengan sisi kiri tubuh.",
        "en": "The name is often translated as \"Left-Handed Hummingbird\" or \"Hummingbird of the South\", because Aztec cosmology associated the south with the left hand side of the body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c09",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "Huitzilopochtli famously avenged the murder of his mother Coatlίcue (or in some versions actually saved her) when he defeated his siblings the 400 Centzonhuitznahuac and Centzonmimizcoa on Mt. Coatepec, the snake mountain.",
      "locator": "Huitzilopochtli as the Sun",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huitzilopochtli terkenal membalas pembunuhan ibunya, Coatlícue (atau dalam beberapa versi justru menyelamatkannya), dengan mengalahkan saudara-saudaranya, 400 Centzonhuitznahuac dan Centzonmimizcoa, di Gunung Coatepec, gunung ular.",
        "en": "Huitzilopochtli famously avenged the murder of his mother Coatlícue (or in some versions actually saved her) when he defeated his siblings, the 400 Centzonhuitznahuac and Centzonmimizcoa, on Mt. Coatepec, the snake mountain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c10",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "Her children were outraged at this development and attacked Coatlίcue but when they decapitated their mother Huitzilopochtli sprang from the corpse with all his weapons in hand and lopped off his sister Coyolxauhqui's head.",
      "locator": "Huitzilopochtli as the Sun",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika anak-anak Coatlícue memenggal ibu mereka, Huitzilopochtli melompat keluar dari jasadnya dengan semua senjata di tangan dan memenggal kepala saudarinya, Coyolxauhqui.",
        "en": "When Coatlícue's children decapitated their mother, Huitzilopochtli sprang from the corpse with all his weapons in hand and lopped off his sister Coyolxauhqui's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c11",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "Huitzilopochtli dismembered her corpse so that her torso fell to the bottom of the mountain and when he slung the head into the skies it became the moon.",
      "locator": "Huitzilopochtli as the Sun",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huitzilopochtli memotong-motong jasad Coyolxauhqui sehingga badannya jatuh ke kaki gunung, dan kepalanya yang ia lemparkan ke langit menjadi bulan.",
        "en": "Huitzilopochtli dismembered her corpse so that her torso fell to the bottom of the mountain, and the head he slung into the skies became the moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c12",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "putting an end to their takeover attempt and dispersing them into the heavens to become the southern and northern constellations respectively.",
      "locator": "Huitzilopochtli as the Sun",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huitzilopochtli menghentikan upaya perebutan kuasa saudara-saudaranya dan menyebarkan mereka ke langit sebagai rasi bintang selatan dan utara.",
        "en": "Huitzilopochtli put an end to his siblings' takeover attempt and dispersed them into the heavens to become the southern and northern constellations respectively."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c13",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "For the Aztecs the tussle between Coyolxauhqui and Huitzilopochtli was thought to reoccur everyday which explained the daily exchange for control of the sky between the sun and moon.",
      "locator": "Huitzilopochtli as the Sun",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bagi bangsa Aztec, pergulatan antara Coyolxauhqui dan Huitzilopochtli dianggap terulang setiap hari, dan itu menjelaskan pergantian harian penguasa langit antara matahari dan bulan.",
        "en": "For the Aztecs the tussle between Coyolxauhqui and Huitzilopochtli was thought to reoccur every day, which explained the daily exchange for control of the sky between the sun and moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c14",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "Huitzilopochtli was believed to be accompanied on the first half of his journey across the sky by the spirits of fallen warriors (who would later return to earth as hummingbirds).",
      "locator": "Huitzilopochtli as the Sun",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada paruh pertama perjalanannya melintasi langit, Huitzilopochtli dipercaya ditemani arwah prajurit yang gugur, yang kelak kembali ke bumi sebagai burung kolibri.",
        "en": "On the first half of his journey across the sky Huitzilopochtli was believed to be accompanied by the spirits of fallen warriors, who would later return to earth as hummingbirds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c15",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "When night fell Huitzilopochtli moved through the Underworld until dawn and he then once more travelled the skies.",
      "locator": "Huitzilopochtli as the Sun",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika malam tiba, Huitzilopochtli bergerak melalui Dunia Bawah sampai fajar, lalu kembali menempuh langit.",
        "en": "When night fell Huitzilopochtli moved through the Underworld until dawn, and he then once more travelled the skies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c16",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "The focal point of worship to Huitzilopochtli was at the Aztec capital where he had a wooden statue and shrine alongside that of Tláloc atop the Temple Mayor pyramid or Hueteocalli.",
      "locator": "Worship of Huitzilopochtli",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di ibu kota Aztec, ia memiliki patung kayu dan kuil di samping milik Tláloc di puncak piramida Temple Mayor atau Hueteocalli.",
        "en": "In the Aztec capital he had a wooden statue and shrine alongside that of Tláloc atop the Temple Mayor pyramid, or Hueteocalli."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c17",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "As Huitzilopochtli was such an important deity he was the beneficiary of human sacrifices whose blood would feed and strengthen the god.",
      "locator": "Worship of Huitzilopochtli",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena begitu pentingnya, Huitzilopochtli menerima korban manusia yang darahnya dipercaya memberi makan dan menguatkan sang dewa.",
        "en": "Because he was such an important deity, Huitzilopochtli was the beneficiary of human sacrifices whose blood would feed and strengthen the god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c18",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "The Méxica, especially, revered Huitzilopochtli as they believed he had led them from the legendary Aztlán cave in the northwest desert on a protracted journey that eventually led to their new capital Tenochtitlán.",
      "locator": "Worship of Huitzilopochtli",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bangsa Méxica sangat memuliakan Huitzilopochtli karena percaya ia memimpin mereka dari gua Aztlán yang legendaris di gurun barat laut dalam perjalanan panjang menuju ibu kota baru mereka, Tenochtitlán.",
        "en": "The Méxica especially revered Huitzilopochtli because they believed he had led them from the legendary Aztlán cave in the northwest desert on a protracted journey that eventually led to their new capital Tenochtitlán."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c19",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "During this migration priests had carried a huge idol of the god who whispered directions, gave the Méxica their name and promised great wealth and prosperity if he was suitably worshipped.",
      "locator": "Worship of Huitzilopochtli",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selama perpindahan itu, para imam membawa arca besar sang dewa yang membisikkan arah, memberi bangsa Méxica nama mereka, dan menjanjikan kekayaan dan kemakmuran bila ia dipuja dengan layak.",
        "en": "During this migration priests carried a huge idol of the god, who whispered directions, gave the Méxica their name and promised great wealth and prosperity if he was suitably worshipped."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c20",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "The great war god instructed that the rebel's heart be thrown as far as possible into Lake Texcoco and where it landed would indicate the place the Méxica should build their new home, the precise spot being marked by an eagle sitting on a prickly-pear cactus.",
      "locator": "Worship of Huitzilopochtli",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sang dewa perang memerintahkan agar jantung pemberontak dilempar sejauh mungkin ke Danau Texcoco; tempat jatuhnya menunjukkan lokasi rumah baru bangsa Méxica, yang ditandai seekor elang di atas kaktus pir berduri.",
        "en": "The great war god instructed that the rebel's heart be thrown as far as possible into Lake Texcoco; where it landed would indicate the place the Méxica should build their new home, marked by an eagle sitting on a prickly-pear cactus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c21",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "Huitzilopochtli may be depicted carrying his snake-shaped spear-thrower (atl-atl) which represents the fire-serpent Xiuhcóatl. He may also carry a shield, hold feathered arrows or darts and be painted with blue arms and legs.",
      "locator": "Representations in Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huitzilopochtli dapat digambarkan membawa pelontar tombak berbentuk ular (atl-atl) yang melambangkan ular api Xiuhcóatl; ia juga bisa membawa perisai atau anak panah berbulu dan dilukis dengan lengan dan kaki biru.",
        "en": "Huitzilopochtli may be depicted carrying his snake-shaped spear-thrower (atl-atl), which represents the fire-serpent Xiuhcóatl; he may also carry a shield or feathered arrows or darts and be painted with blue arms and legs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c22",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "The god could be symbolised by either a hummingbird (huitzilin) - whose feathers he wore in his helmet - or an eagle sitting on a prickly pear cactus holding a snake in one of its talons, an image seen today in the national flag of Mexico.",
      "locator": "Representations in Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sang dewa dapat dilambangkan dengan kolibri (huitzilin), yang bulunya ia kenakan di helmnya, atau elang di atas kaktus pir berduri yang mencengkeram ular, gambar yang kini terlihat pada bendera nasional Meksiko.",
        "en": "The god could be symbolised by a hummingbird (huitzilin), whose feathers he wore in his helmet, or by an eagle sitting on a prickly pear cactus holding a snake, an image seen today on the national flag of Mexico."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c23",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "In Aztec mythology Huitzilopochtli was the son of Omecίhuatl and Ometecuhtli, respectively, the female and male aspects of the androgynous primordial god Ometeotl. ... In an alternative version, the god is the offspring of Coatlίcue, the supreme earth goddess.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Aztec, Huitzilopochtli adalah putra Omecíhuatl dan Ometecuhtli; dalam versi lain ia anak Coatlícue, dewi bumi tertinggi.",
        "en": "In Aztec mythology Huitzilopochtli was the son of Omecíhuatl and Ometecuhtli; in an alternative version he is the offspring of Coatlícue, the supreme earth goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c24",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "Huitzilopochtli was also considered the brother of those other great Mesoamerican gods Quetzalcoatl, Tezcatlipoca and Xipe Totec.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Huitzilopochtli juga dianggap saudara dewa-dewa besar Mesoamerika lainnya: Quetzalcoatl, Tezcatlipoca, dan Xipe Totec.",
        "en": "Huitzilopochtli was also considered the brother of the other great Mesoamerican gods Quetzalcoatl, Tezcatlipoca and Xipe Totec."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c25",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s2",
      "quote": "he was also celebrated during the month of Toxcatl when an effigy or tzoalli made from dough using the amaranth plant and dressed as Huitzilopochtli was taken to the god's temple in a great procession and then eaten.",
      "locator": "Worship of Huitzilopochtli",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga dirayakan pada bulan Toxcatl, ketika sebuah patung adonan amaranta (tzoalli) berpakaian Huitzilopochtli diarak ke kuilnya lalu dimakan.",
        "en": "He was also celebrated in the month of Toxcatl, when an effigy (tzoalli) made from amaranth dough and dressed as Huitzilopochtli was taken to the god's temple in a great procession and then eaten."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c26",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "Originally, he was of little importance to the Nahuas, but after the rise of the Aztecs, Tlacaelel reformed their religion and put Huitzilopochtli at the same level as Quetzalcoatl, Tlaloc, and Tezcatlipoca, making him a solar god.",
      "locator": "History",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mulanya ia kurang penting bagi bangsa Nahua, tetapi setelah bangkitnya bangsa Aztec, Tlacaelel mereformasi agama mereka dan menempatkannya sejajar dengan Quetzalcoatl, Tlaloc, dan Tezcatlipoca sebagai dewa matahari.",
        "en": "Originally he was of little importance to the Nahuas, but after the rise of the Aztecs, Tlacaelel reformed their religion and put him at the same level as Quetzalcoatl, Tlaloc and Tezcatlipoca, making him a solar god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c27",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "Huitzilopochtli is seen as the sun in mythology, while his many male siblings are perceived as the stars and his sister as the moon. In the Aztec worldview, this is the reason why the Sun is constantly chasing the Moon and stars. It is also why it was so important to provide tribute for Huitzilopochtli as sustenance for the Sun.",
      "locator": "Origin stories",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam pandangan dunia Aztec, matahari terus mengejar bulan dan bintang, dan karena itu penting memberi persembahan kepada Huitzilopochtli sebagai santapan bagi Matahari.",
        "en": "In the Aztec worldview the Sun is constantly chasing the Moon and stars, which is why it was so important to provide tribute for Huitzilopochtli as sustenance for the Sun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "huitzilopochtli-c28",
      "source_id": "huitzilopochtli-s1",
      "quote": "Together, Huitzilopochtli and Quetzalcoatl created fire, the first male and female humans, the Earth, and the Sun.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu kisah asal-usul, Huitzilopochtli dan Quetzalcoatl bersama-sama menciptakan api, manusia laki-laki dan perempuan pertama, Bumi, dan Matahari.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Viracocha adalah dewa pencipta dan dewa tertinggi dalam mitologi pra-Inca dan Inca di wilayah Andes. Menurut mitos ia berwujud manusia berjanggut dan memerintahkan pembangunan Tiwanaku.",
    "en": "Viracocha is the creator and supreme deity in pre-Inca and Inca mythology in the Andes region. According to myth he had human appearance, was bearded, and ordered the construction of Tiwanaku.",

      "viracocha-c01",
      "viracocha-c02",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Viracocha adalah dewa pencipta dan dewa tertinggi dalam mitologi pra-Inca dan Inca di wilayah Andes. Ia dianggap membentuk bumi, langit, matahari, bulan, dan semua makhluk hidup, dan ia dipuja sebagai dewa matahari dan badai. Menurut sumber lain, ia adalah dewa terpenting dalam jajaran dewa Inca dan sangat lekat dengan laut.",
      "en": "Viracocha is the creator and supreme deity in pre-Inca and Inca mythology in the Andes region. He is said to have formed the earth, heavens, sun, moon and all living beings, and he was worshipped as god of the sun and of storms. According to another source, he was the most important deity in the Inca pantheon and intimately associated with the sea.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "viracocha-c01",
        "viracocha-c05",
        "viracocha-c22",
        "viracocha-c23"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Menurut mitos, Viracocha berwujud manusia dan umumnya dianggap berjanggut. Dalam versi yang dicatat dari sumber Spanyol, ia bangkit dari Danau Titicaca pada masa kegelapan, membuat matahari, bulan, dan bintang, lalu menciptakan raksasa yang kemudian dimusnahkan dengan banjir sebelum membuat manusia yang lebih baik. Dalam penuturan lain, manusia dibuat dari tanah liat setelah raksasa batu dihukum dengan banjir. Kisah \"dewa putih\" dianggap sebagian besar sarjana modern sebagai rekaan Spanyol pascapenaklukan, karena kulit putihnya tidak disebut dalam legenda asli bangsa Inca.",
      "en": "According to myth, Viracocha had human appearance and was generally considered bearded. In a version recorded from Spanish sources, he rose from Lake Titicaca in the time of darkness, made the sun, moon and stars, and created giants who were destroyed by a flood before he made better humans. In another telling, humans were made of clay after the stone giants were punished with a flood. Most modern scholars regard the \"white god\" story as a Spanish invention made after the conquest, since his whiteness is absent from the original Inca legends.",

      "id": "Setelah selesai berkarya, ia dipercaya berkelana, mengajar manusia, dan membawa seni peradaban, lalu menuju barat melintasi Pasifik dan berjanji suatu hari kembali. Dalam perjalanannya ia berpakaian pengemis dan dibantu dua putra atau saudara, Imaymana Viracocha dan Tocapo Viracocha.",
      "en": "When his work was finished he was believed to have travelled, teaching humanity and bringing the civilised arts, before heading west across the Pacific and promising one day to return.     {
      "id": "viracocha-s2",
      "url": "https://www.worldhistory.org/Viracocha/",
      "title": "Viracocha",
      "author": "Mark Cartwright",
      "publisher": "World History Encyclopedia",
      "published": "2014-02-23",
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-06"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c01",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "Viracocha (also Wiraqocha, Huiracocha; Quechua Wiraqucha) is the creator and supreme deity in the pre-Inca and Inca mythology in the Andes region of South America.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Viracocha adalah dewa pencipta tertinggi dalam mitologi Inca di Andes.",
        "en": "Viracocha is the creator and supreme deity in pre-Inca and Inca mythology in the Andes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c02",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "According to the myth Viracocha had human appearance and was generally considered as bearded.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut mitos, Viracocha berwujud manusia dan umumnya digambarkan berjanggut.",
        "en": "According to myth, Viracocha had human appearance and was generally considered bearded."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c03",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "According to the myth he ordered the construction of Tiwanaku.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut mitos, ia memerintahkan pembangunan Tiwanaku.",
        "en": "According to the myth he ordered the construction of Tiwanaku."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c04",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "Such compound names include Ticsi Viracocha (T'iqsi Wiraqocha), Contiti Viracocha, and, occasionally, Kon-Tiki Viracocha (the source of the name of Thor Heyerdahl's raft).",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama majemuk Viracocha antara lain Ticsi Viracocha dan Contiti Viracocha, serta kadang Kon-Tiki Viracocha, yang menjadi sumber nama rakit Thor Heyerdahl.",
        "en": "Compound names of Viracocha include Ticsi Viracocha and Contiti Viracocha and, occasionally, Kon-Tiki Viracocha, the source of the name of Thor Heyerdahl's raft."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c05",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "Considered the creator god he was the father of all other Inca gods and it was he who formed the earth, heavens, sun, moon and all living beings.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai dewa pencipta, ia dianggap ayah semua dewa Inca lainnya dan dialah yang membentuk bumi, langit, matahari, bulan, dan semua makhluk hidup.",
        "en": "Considered the creator god, he was the father of all other Inca gods and formed the earth, heavens, sun, moon and all living beings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c06",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "When he finished his work he was believed to have travelled far and wide teaching humanity and bringing the civilised arts before he headed west across the Pacific, never to be seen again but promising one day to return.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah menyelesaikan karyanya, ia dipercaya berkelana jauh mengajar manusia dan membawa seni peradaban, lalu menuju barat melintasi Pasifik dan tak terlihat lagi, tetapi berjanji suatu hari kembali.",
        "en": "When he finished his work he was believed to have travelled far and wide teaching humanity and bringing the civilised arts, before heading west across the Pacific, never to be seen again but promising one day to return."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c07",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "These other names, perhaps used because the god's real name was too sacred to be spoken, included Ilya (light), Ticci (beginning), and Wiraqoca Pacayacaciq (instructor).",
      "locator": "Alternative Names",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama-nama lain, yang mungkin dipakai karena nama aslinya terlalu sakral untuk diucapkan, mencakup Ilya (cahaya), Ticci (permulaan), dan Wiraqoca Pacayacaciq (pengajar).",
        "en": "Other names, perhaps used because the god's real name was too sacred to be spoken, included Ilya (light), Ticci (beginning) and Wiraqoca Pacayacaciq (instructor)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c08",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "It was believed that human beings were actually Viracocha's second attempt at living creatures as he first created a race of giants from stone in the age of darkness.",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Manusia dipercaya merupakan percobaan kedua Viracocha menciptakan makhluk hidup, karena ia lebih dulu menciptakan ras raksasa dari batu pada zaman kegelapan.",
        "en": "Human beings were believed to be Viracocha's second attempt at living creatures, as he first created a race of giants from stone in the age of darkness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c09",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "However, these giants proved unruly and it became necessary for Viracocha to punish them by sending a great flood. In the legend all these giants except two then returned to their original stone form and several could still be seen in much later times standing imposingly at sites such as Tiahuanaco",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para raksasa itu ternyata membangkang sehingga Viracocha menghukum mereka dengan banjir besar; semuanya kecuali dua kembali menjadi batu, dan beberapa masih terlihat di tempat seperti Tiahuanaco.",
        "en": "The giants proved unruly, so Viracocha punished them with a great flood; all but two returned to stone form, and several could still be seen at sites such as Tiahuanaco."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c10",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "Then Viracocha created men and women but this time he used clay. He also gave them such gifts as clothes, language, agriculture and the arts and then created all animals.",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemudian Viracocha menciptakan laki-laki dan perempuan, kali ini dari tanah liat, memberi mereka pakaian, bahasa, pertanian, dan seni, lalu menciptakan semua hewan.",
        "en": "Viracocha then created men and women, this time from clay, gave them gifts such as clothes, language, agriculture and the arts, and then created all animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c11",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "Viracocha's decision to create the sun, moon and stars and so bring light to the world. These heavenly bodies were created from islands in Lake Titicaca.",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Viracocha menciptakan matahari, bulan, dan bintang sehingga membawa cahaya ke dunia; benda-benda langit itu dibuat dari pulau-pulau di Danau Titicaca.",
        "en": "Viracocha created the sun, moon and stars and so brought light to the world; these heavenly bodies were created from islands in Lake Titicaca."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c12",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "Viracocha then set off to spread his civilizing knowledge around the world and for this he dressed as a beggar and assumed such names as Con Ticci Viracocha (also spelt Kon-Tiki), Atun-Viracocha and Contiti Viracocha Pachayachachic.",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Viracocha lalu berkelana menyebarkan pengetahuan beradab ke seluruh dunia dengan berpakaian pengemis dan memakai nama seperti Con Ticci Viracocha (juga Kon-Tiki), Atun-Viracocha, dan Contiti Viracocha Pachayachachic.",
        "en": "Viracocha then set off to spread his civilizing knowledge around the world, dressed as a beggar and assuming names such as Con Ticci Viracocha (also spelt Kon-Tiki), Atun-Viracocha and Contiti Viracocha Pachayachachic."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c13",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "He was assissted on his travels by two sons or brothers called Imaymana Viracocha and Tocapo Viracocha.",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam perjalanannya ia dibantu dua putra atau saudara bernama Imaymana Viracocha dan Tocapo Viracocha.",
        "en": "On his travels he was assisted by two sons or brothers called Imaymana Viracocha and Tocapo Viracocha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c14",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "Ending up at Manta (in Ecuador), Viracocha then walked across the waters of the Pacific (in some versions he sails a raft) heading into the west but promising to return one day",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setibanya di Manta (di Ekuador), Viracocha berjalan di atas perairan Pasifik (dalam beberapa versi ia berlayar dengan rakit) menuju barat sambil berjanji akan kembali suatu hari.",
        "en": "Ending up at Manta (in Ecuador), Viracocha walked across the waters of the Pacific (in some versions he sails a raft) heading west, promising to return one day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c15",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "Viracocha was actually worshipped by the pre-Inca of Peru before being incorporated into the Inca pantheon.",
      "locator": "Worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Viracocha sudah dipuja oleh bangsa pra-Inca di Peru sebelum dimasukkan ke dalam jajaran dewa Inca.",
        "en": "Viracocha was worshipped by the pre-Inca of Peru before being incorporated into the Inca pantheon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c16",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "As other Inca gods were more important for the daily life of common people, Viracocha was principally worshipped by the nobility, and then usually in times of political crisis.",
      "locator": "Worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena dewa Inca lain lebih penting bagi kehidupan sehari-hari rakyat biasa, Viracocha terutama dipuja oleh kaum bangsawan, biasanya pada masa krisis politik.",
        "en": "As other Inca gods were more important for the daily life of common people, Viracocha was principally worshipped by the nobility, and then usually in times of political crisis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c17",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "Worshipped at the Inca capital of Cuzco, Viracocha also had temples and statues dedicated to him at Caha and Urcos and sacrifices of humans (including children) and, quite often, llamas, were made to the god on important ceremonial occasions.",
      "locator": "Worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di ibu kota Inca, Cuzco, Viracocha dipuja, dan juga memiliki kuil serta patung di Caha dan Urcos; korban manusia (termasuk anak-anak) dan cukup sering llama dipersembahkan pada upacara penting.",
        "en": "Worshipped at the Inca capital of Cuzco, Viracocha also had temples and statues at Caha and Urcos, and sacrifices of humans (including children) and, quite often, llamas were made to the god on important ceremonial occasions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c18",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "In art Viracocha is often depicted as an old bearded man wearing a long robe and supported by a staff.",
      "locator": "Viracocha in Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam seni, Viracocha sering digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua berjanggut berjubah panjang yang ditopang tongkat.",
        "en": "In art Viracocha is often depicted as an old bearded man wearing a long robe and supported by a staff."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c19",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "One of his earliest representations may be the weeping statue at the ruins of Tiwanaku, close to Lake Titicaca, the traditional Inca site where all things were first created. Here, sculpted on the lintel of a massive gateway, the god holds thunderbolts in each hand and wears a crown with rays of the sun whilst his tears represent the rain.",
      "locator": "Viracocha in Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada ambang gerbang besar di Tiwanaku, sang dewa dipahat memegang petir di kedua tangan dan bermahkota sinar matahari, sementara air matanya melambangkan hujan.",
        "en": "Sculpted on the lintel of a massive gateway at Tiwanaku, the god holds thunderbolts in each hand and wears a crown with rays of the sun, while his tears represent the rain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c20",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "In the Quechuan languages, tiqsi means \"origin\" or \"beginning\", wira means fat, and qucha means lake, sea, or reservoir.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam rumpun bahasa Quechua, tiqsi berarti \"asal\" atau \"permulaan\", wira berarti lemak, dan qucha berarti danau, laut, atau waduk.",
        "en": "In the Quechuan languages, tiqsi means \"origin\" or \"beginning\", wira means fat, and qucha means lake, sea or reservoir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c21",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "Some people state that Wiraqucha could mean \"Fat (or foam) of the sea\", etymology that has been discarded for grammatical considerations (constituent order in Quechua) at least since Inca Garcilaso.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang menyatakan Wiraqucha dapat berarti \"lemak (atau busa) laut\", tetapi etimologi ini ditinggalkan karena pertimbangan tata bahasa (urutan unsur dalam bahasa Quechua) setidaknya sejak Inca Garcilaso.",
        "en": "Some people state that Wiraqucha could mean \"Fat (or foam) of the sea\", an etymology discarded for grammatical considerations (constituent order in Quechua) at least since Inca Garcilaso."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c22",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "Viracocha created the universe, sun, moon, and stars, time (by commanding the sun to move over the sky) and civilization itself. Viracocha was worshipped as god of the sun and of storms.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Viracocha menciptakan alam semesta, matahari, bulan, bintang, waktu (dengan memerintahkan matahari bergerak di langit), dan peradaban itu sendiri, serta dipuja sebagai dewa matahari dan badai.",
        "en": "Viracocha created the universe, sun, moon and stars, time (by commanding the sun to move over the sky) and civilization itself, and was worshipped as god of the sun and of storms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c23",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "Viracocha was the most important deity in the Inca pantheon and seen as the creator of all things, or the substance from which all things are created, and intimately associated with the sea.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Viracocha adalah dewa terpenting dalam jajaran dewa Inca, dipandang sebagai pencipta segala sesuatu atau bahan dari mana segala sesuatu diciptakan, dan sangat lekat dengan laut.",
        "en": "Viracocha was the most important deity in the Inca pantheon, seen as the creator of all things or the substance from which all things are created, and intimately associated with the sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c24",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "According to a myth recorded by Juan de Betanzos, Viracocha rose from Lake Titicaca (or sometimes the cave of Paqariq Tampu) during the time of darkness to bring forth light.",
      "locator": "Cosmogony according to Spanish accounts",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut mitos yang dicatat Juan de Betanzos, Viracocha bangkit dari Danau Titicaca (atau kadang gua Paqariq Tampu) pada masa kegelapan untuk memunculkan cahaya.",
        "en": "According to a myth recorded by Juan de Betanzos, Viracocha rose from Lake Titicaca (or sometimes the cave of Paqariq Tampu) during the time of darkness to bring forth light."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c25",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "He made mankind by breathing into stones, but his first creation were brainless giants that displeased him. So, he destroyed them with a flood and made humans, beings who were better than the giants, from smaller stones.",
      "locator": "Cosmogony according to Spanish accounts",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam catatan itu, ia membuat manusia dengan menghembuskan napas ke batu; ciptaan pertamanya adalah raksasa tak berotak yang tidak menyenangkannya, sehingga ia memusnahkan mereka dengan banjir dan membuat manusia yang lebih baik dari batu yang lebih kecil.",
        "en": "In that account he made mankind by breathing into stones; his first creation was brainless giants that displeased him, so he destroyed them with a flood and made better humans from smaller stones."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c26",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "In one legend he had one son, Inti, and two daughters, Mama Killa and Pachamama.",
      "locator": "Cosmogony according to Spanish accounts",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu legenda, ia memiliki satu putra, Inti, dan dua putri, Mama Killa dan Pachamama.",
        "en": "In one legend he had one son, Inti, and two daughters, Mama Killa and Pachamama."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c27",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "In this legend, he destroyed the people around Lake Titicaca with a Great Flood called Unu Pachakuti, lasting 60 days and 60 nights, saving two to bring civilization to the rest of the world.",
      "locator": "Cosmogony according to Spanish accounts",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda itu, ia memusnahkan orang-orang di sekitar Danau Titicaca dengan Banjir Besar bernama Unu Pachakuti selama 60 hari 60 malam, dan menyelamatkan dua orang untuk membawa peradaban ke seluruh dunia.",
        "en": "In that legend he destroyed the people around Lake Titicaca with a Great Flood called Unu Pachakuti, lasting 60 days and 60 nights, saving two to bring civilization to the rest of the world."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c28",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "The whiteness of Viracocha is however not mentioned in the native authentic legends of the Incas and most modern scholars therefore had considered the \"white god\" story to be a post-conquest Spanish invention.",
      "locator": "Controversy over \"white god\"",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Warna kulit putih Viracocha tidak disebut dalam legenda asli bangsa Inca, sehingga sebagian besar sarjana modern menganggap kisah \"dewa putih\" sebagai rekaan Spanyol pascapenaklukan.",
        "en": "The whiteness of Viracocha is not mentioned in the native authentic legends of the Incas, and most modern scholars therefore considered the \"white god\" story a post-conquest Spanish invention."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c29",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s1",
      "quote": "The Colombian myth of Bochica who has a similar role as creator and civilizer as Viracocha",
      "locator": "See also",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos Kolombia tentang Bochica memiliki peran serupa sebagai pencipta dan pembawa peradaban seperti Viracocha.",
        "en": "The Colombian myth of Bochica has a similar role as creator and civilizer as Viracocha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c30",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "The god's name was also assumed by the king known as Viracocha Inca (died 1438 CE) and this may also be the time when the god was formally added to the family of Inca gods.",
      "locator": "Worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama sang dewa juga dipakai oleh raja yang dikenal sebagai Viracocha Inca (wafat 1438 M), dan mungkin saat itulah sang dewa secara resmi ditambahkan ke keluarga dewa Inca.",
        "en": "The god's name was also assumed by the king known as Viracocha Inca (died 1438 CE), and this may also be the time when the god was formally added to the family of Inca gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "viracocha-c31",
      "source_id": "viracocha-s2",
      "quote": "The god was not always well received despite the knowledge he imparted, sometimes even suffering stones thrown at him.",
      "locator": "The Creator God",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sang dewa tidak selalu disambut baik meskipun pengetahuan yang diberikannya, bahkan kadang dilempari batu.",
        "en": "The god was not always well received despite the knowledge he imparted, sometimes even suffering stones thrown at him."
      }
    }
  ],

  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Tidak ada gambar baru ditambahkan sesuai instruksi pengayaan; entri ini tidak memiliki gambar."

    {

    {
      "id": "Pachamama atau Mama Pacha adalah dewa Andes yang mewakili ruang-waktu dan dipuja oleh bangsa-bangsa Andes. Dalam mitologi dan agama Inca, ia dipandang sebagai dewi ibu yang terkait kehidupan, kesuburan, dan alam, dan dianggap hadir di mana-mana serta berdaya cipta untuk menopang kehidupan. Empat prinsip kosmologis Quechua (Air, Bumi, Matahari, dan Bulan) menganggapnya asal utama, dan dari kesuburannya kosmos dikatakan lahir dan bumi disuburkan.",
      "en": "Pachamama, also called Mama Pacha, is an Andean deity who stands for space-time and is honoured by Andean peoples. Inca mythology and religion treat her as a mother goddess connected with life, fertility and nature, thought to be ever-present and able to create and sustain life. Four cosmological Quechua principles (Water, Earth, Sun and Moon) trace their origin to her, and the cosmos is said to be born from her fertility, with the earth made fertile.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pachamama-c01",
        "pachamama-c05",
        "pachamama-c04",
        "pachamama-c02",
        "pachamama-c14"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Ia biasanya dewi yang penuh kasih dan murah hati, tetapi dapat menjadi mengerikan dan merusak ketika tersinggung. Para imam mempersembahkan llama, marmut, dan pakaian mini yang dibakar kepadanya. Ia juga digambarkan sebagai dewi purba yang tidak membutuhkan kuil khusus.",
      "en": "She is normally loving and generous, yet can turn terrible and destructive when offended. Pachamama was commonly worshipped in her earthly form, Allpamama or Mama Allpa; her shrines were sacred rocks or the trunks of legendary trees, and she was pictured as a woman carrying potato or coca-leaf harvests. Priests offered llamas, guinea pigs and small burned garments to her. She is also described as an ancient goddess who needs no special temples.",

      "en": "Her worship proceeds through offerings.       "id": "Program Peru pada Festival Folklife 2015 memakai nama Pachamama, \"Ibu Bumi\" dalam bahasa Quechua dan Aymara.       "en": "The Peru program of the 2015 Folklife Festival took its name from Pachamama, \"Mother Earth\" in Quechua and Aymara.       "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi dan agama Inca, ia adalah dewa bertipe \"dewi ibu\" yang mewakili energi universal yang menghubungkan segalanya, terkait ruang, waktu, alam semesta, totalitas, kehidupan, kesuburan, dan alam.",

      "source_id": "pachamama-s1",
      "quote": "She is attributed with the role of mother of the world, from whom the material and spiritual sustenance of human beings comes.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "pachamama-c07",
      "source_id": "pachamama-s1",
      "quote": "In various myths, Pachamama is described as the wife of Pachacámac and mother of Mama Quilla (the goddess of the moon) and Inti (the god of the sun).",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam berbagai mitos, Pachamama digambarkan sebagai istri Pachacámac dan ibu Mama Quilla (dewi bulan) dan Inti (dewa matahari).",
        "en": "In various myths, Pachamama is described as the wife of Pachacámac and mother of Mama Quilla (the goddess of the moon) and Inti (the god of the sun)."

    },
    {
      "id": "pachamama-c08",
      "source_id": "pachamama-s1",
      "quote": "Pachamama was commonly worshipped in her earthly manifestation or form called Allpamama or Mama Allpa (goddess of the earth), her shrines consisted of sacred rocks or the trunks of legendary trees, and artists depicted her as a woman bearing harvests of potatoes or coca leaves.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pachamama umum dipuja dalam wujud duniawinya yang disebut Allpamama atau Mama Allpa (dewi bumi); kuilnya berupa batu keramat atau batang pohon legendaris, dan seniman menggambarkannya sebagai perempuan pembawa hasil panen kentang atau daun koka.",
        "en": "Pachamama was commonly worshipped in her earthly form called Allpamama or Mama Allpa (goddess of the earth); her shrines were sacred rocks or the trunks of legendary trees, and artists depicted her as a woman bearing harvests of potatoes or coca leaves."

    },
    {
      "id": "pachamama-c09",
      "source_id": "pachamama-s1",
      "quote": "Priests sacrifice offerings of llamas, cuy (guinea pigs), and elaborate, miniature, burned garments to her.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Priests sacrifice offerings of llamas, cuy (guinea pigs) and elaborate miniature burned garments to her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pachamama-c10",
      "source_id": "pachamama-s1",
      "quote": "She is normally a loving and generous goddess, but she can be terrible, cruel, and destructive when she is upset or feels hurt, capable of destroying men and everything above or within her.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia biasanya dewi yang penuh kasih dan murah hati, tetapi bisa mengerikan, kejam, dan merusak ketika marah atau merasa terluka, mampu menghancurkan manusia dan segala yang ada di atas atau di dalamnya.",
        "en": "She is normally a loving and generous goddess, but she can be terrible, cruel and destructive when upset or hurt, capable of destroying men and everything above or within her."

    },
    {
      "id": "pachamama-c11",
      "source_id": "pachamama-s1",
      "quote": "Pachamama is an ancient and primordial goddess who does not need temples or specific places of worship, although she likes springs, simply because she is everywhere and at all times.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pachamama adalah dewi purba dan primordial yang tidak membutuhkan kuil atau tempat ibadah khusus, meskipun ia menyukai mata air, karena ia ada di mana-mana dan sepanjang waktu.",
        "en": "Pachamama is an ancient and primordial goddess who does not need temples or specific places of worship, although she likes springs, simply because she is everywhere and at all times."

      "source_id": "pachamama-s1",
      "quote": "The word Pachamama comes from the Quechua language and is composed of two terms: Pacha: a complex word meaning 'world, universe, space-time, totality, era, cosmos'.",

      "statement": {

        "en": "The word Pachamama comes from the Quechua language and has two parts; the first, pacha, is a complex word meaning 'world, universe, space-time, totality, era, cosmos'."
