      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam beberapa penggambaran, wendigo dilukiskan sebagai sosok raksasa berhati es dengan bau busuk atau hawa dingin tiba-tiba.",
        "en": "In some representations, the wendigo is described as a giant humanoid with a heart of ice signaled by foul stench or chill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c05",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "is a mythological creature or evil spirit originating from Algonquian folklore.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wendigo adalah makhluk mitologis atau roh jahat yang berasal dari cerita rakyat Algonquian.",
        "en": "The wendigo is a mythological creature or evil spirit originating from Algonquian folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c06",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "The contemporary English word wendigo is a loanword from multiple origins. It is partially a borrowing from Cree (wīhtikōw ) and partially a borrowing from Ojibwe (wiindigoo ), both Algonquian languages.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Inggris wendigo sebagian merupakan serapan dari bahasa Cree (wīhtikōw) dan sebagian dari bahasa Ojibwe (wiindigoo), keduanya bahasa Algonquian.",
        "en": "The English word wendigo is partly a borrowing from Cree (wīhtikōw) and partly from Ojibwe (wiindigoo), both Algonquian languages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c07",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "The form wendigo was popularized in the English language by Algernon Blackwood, who in 1910 published the novella The Wendigo.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk wendigo dipopulerkan dalam bahasa Inggris oleh Algernon Blackwood, yang pada 1910 menerbitkan novela The Wendigo.",
        "en": "The form wendigo was popularized in English by Algernon Blackwood, who in 1910 published the novella The Wendigo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c08",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "common to all these cultures is the view that the wendigo is a malevolent, cannibalistic, supernatural being. They were strongly associated with the north, winter, cold, famine, and starvation.",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pandangan yang sama di semua budaya itu adalah bahwa wendigo makhluk gaib jahat pemakan manusia, yang sangat dikaitkan dengan utara, musim dingin, hawa dingin, kelaparan, dan paceklik.",
        "en": "Common to all these cultures is the view that the wendigo is a malevolent, cannibalistic, supernatural being, strongly associated with the north, winter, cold, famine and starvation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c09",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "In Ojibwe, Eastern Cree, Westmain Swampy Cree, Naskapi, and Innu lore, wendigos are often described as giants that are many times larger than human beings",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Ojibwe, Cree Timur, Cree Rawa Westmain, Naskapi, dan Innu, wendigo sering digambarkan sebagai raksasa yang berkali-kali lipat lebih besar daripada manusia.",
        "en": "In Ojibwe, Eastern Cree, Westmain Swampy Cree, Naskapi and Innu lore, wendigos are often described as giants many times larger than human beings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c10",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "Whenever a wendigo ate another person, it would grow in proportion to the meal it had just eaten, so it could never be full.",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setiap kali wendigo memakan seseorang, ia tumbuh sebanding dengan santapannya sehingga tidak pernah kenyang.",
        "en": "Whenever a wendigo ate another person it would grow in proportion to the meal, so it could never be full."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c11",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "The wendigo is seen as the embodiment of gluttony, greed, and excess.",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wendigo dipandang sebagai perwujudan kerakusan, keserakahan, dan kelebihan.",
        "en": "The wendigo is seen as the embodiment of gluttony, greed and excess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c12",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "A wendigo need not lose the human's powers of cognition or speech and in some depictions may clearly communicate with its prospective victims or even threaten or taunt them.",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wendigo tidak harus kehilangan daya pikir atau bicara manusia, dan dalam sebagian gambaran ia dapat berkomunikasi dengan calon korbannya, bahkan mengancam atau mengejek mereka.",
        "en": "A wendigo need not lose the human's powers of cognition or speech, and in some depictions may communicate with prospective victims or even threaten or taunt them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c13",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "A specimen of folk story collected in the early 20th century by Lottie Chicogquaw Marsden, an ethnographer of the Chippewas of Rama First Nation, in which a wendigo also exhibits tool use, an ability to survive partial dismemberment, and autocannibalism",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah cerita rakyat yang dikumpulkan pada awal abad ke-20 oleh Lottie Chicogquaw Marsden, etnografer Chippewas of Rama First Nation, menampilkan wendigo yang memakai alat, mampu bertahan setelah tubuhnya sebagian terpotong, dan memakan dirinya sendiri.",
        "en": "A folk story collected in the early 20th century by Lottie Chicogquaw Marsden, an ethnographer of the Chippewas of Rama First Nation, shows a wendigo using tools, surviving partial dismemberment and practising autocannibalism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c14",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "One time long ago a big Windigo stole an Indian boy, but the boy was too thin, so the Windigo didn't eat him up right away, but he travelled with the Indian boy waiting for him till he'd get fat.",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita itu, seorang Windigo besar menculik seorang anak laki-laki tetapi tidak langsung memakannya karena anak itu terlalu kurus, sehingga ia membawanya berkelana menunggu anak itu gemuk.",
        "en": "In that story a big Windigo stole a boy but did not eat him right away because the boy was too thin, so he travelled with him waiting till he got fat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c15",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "The Indians then killed him and cut him to pieces. This was the end of this Giant Windigo.",
      "locator": "Folklore",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada akhir cerita, orang-orang membunuh Windigo itu dan memotongnya menjadi beberapa bagian; itulah akhir Windigo raksasa itu.",
        "en": "At the end of the story the people killed the Windigo and cut him to pieces; that was the end of this Giant Windigo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c16",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "In some traditions, humans overpowered by greed could turn into wendigos; the myth thus served as a method of encouraging cooperation and moderation. Other sources say wendigos were created when a human resorted to cannibalism to survive.",
      "locator": "Human cannibalism",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian tradisi, manusia yang dikuasai keserakahan dapat menjadi wendigo, sehingga mitos ini mendorong kerja sama dan kesederhanaan; sumber lain menyebut wendigo tercipta ketika manusia memakan sesamanya demi bertahan hidup.",
        "en": "In some traditions humans overpowered by greed could turn into wendigos, so the myth encouraged cooperation and moderation; other sources say wendigos were created when a human resorted to cannibalism to survive."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c17",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "Humans could also turn into wendigos by being in contact with them for too long.",
      "locator": "Human cannibalism",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Manusia juga dapat menjadi wendigo karena terlalu lama berhubungan dengan mereka.",
        "en": "Humans could also turn into wendigos by being in contact with them for too long."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c18",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "Among the Assiniboine, the Cree, and the Ojibwe, a satirical ceremonial dance is sometimes performed during times of famine to reinforce the seriousness of the wendigo taboo.",
      "locator": "Taboo reinforcement ceremony",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di kalangan Assiniboine, Cree, dan Ojibwe, tarian upacara satir kadang dipentaskan pada masa paceklik untuk menegaskan keseriusan pantangan wendigo.",
        "en": "Among the Assiniboine, Cree and Ojibwe a satirical ceremonial dance is sometimes performed during famine to reinforce the seriousness of the wendigo taboo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c19",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "The last known wendigo ceremony conducted in the United States was at Lake Windigo of Star Island of Cass Lake, within the Leech Lake Indian Reservation in northern Minnesota.",
      "locator": "Taboo reinforcement ceremony",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Upacara wendigo terakhir yang diketahui di Amerika Serikat dilakukan di Lake Windigo di Star Island, Cass Lake, dalam Reservasi Indian Leech Lake di Minnesota utara.",
        "en": "The last known wendigo ceremony in the United States was held at Lake Windigo on Star Island of Cass Lake, within the Leech Lake Indian Reservation in northern Minnesota."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c20",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "In modern psychiatry, the disorder known as \"Wendigo psychosis\" is characterized by symptoms such as an intense craving for human flesh and fear of becoming a cannibal. Wendigo psychosis is described as a culture-bound syndrome.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam psikiatri modern, gangguan yang dikenal sebagai \"psikosis wendigo\" dicirikan oleh gejala seperti nafsu kuat terhadap daging manusia dan ketakutan menjadi kanibal, dan digambarkan sebagai sindrom terikat budaya.",
        "en": "In modern psychiatry the disorder known as \"Wendigo psychosis\" is characterized by symptoms such as an intense craving for human flesh and fear of becoming a cannibal, and is described as a culture-bound syndrome."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c21",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "Some researchers argued that, essentially, Wendigo psychosis was a fabrication, the result of naïve anthropologists taking stories related to them at face value without observation.",
      "locator": "Psychosis",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian peneliti berpendapat bahwa psikosis wendigo pada dasarnya rekaan, hasil antropolog naif yang menelan mentah-mentah cerita tanpa pengamatan.",
        "en": "Some researchers argued that Wendigo psychosis was essentially a fabrication, the result of naive anthropologists taking stories at face value without observation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c22",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "Others cited several credible eyewitness accounts by Algonquians and others, as evidence that wendigo psychosis was a factual historical phenomenon.",
      "locator": "Psychosis",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti lain mengutip beberapa kesaksian saksi mata yang kredibel dari orang Algonquian dan lainnya sebagai bukti bahwa psikosis wendigo adalah fenomena sejarah yang nyata.",
        "en": "Others cited several credible eyewitness accounts by Algonquians and others as evidence that wendigo psychosis was a factual historical phenomenon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c23",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "As a concept, the wendigo can apply to any person, idea, or movement infected by a corrosive drive toward self-aggrandizing greed and excessive consumption, traits that sow disharmony and destruction if left unchecked.",
      "locator": "As a concept or metaphor",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai konsep, wendigo dapat merujuk pada orang, gagasan, atau gerakan mana pun yang terjangkit dorongan merusak berupa keserakahan yang mementingkan diri dan konsumsi berlebihan, sifat yang menabur ketidakselarasan dan kehancuran bila dibiarkan.",
        "en": "As a concept the wendigo can apply to any person, idea or movement infected by a corrosive drive toward self-aggrandizing greed and excessive consumption, traits that sow disharmony and destruction if left unchecked."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c24",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "The wechuge is a similar being that appears in the legends of the Athabaskan people of the Northwest Pacific Coast. It too is cannibalistic; however, it is characterized as enlightened with ancestral insights.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wechuge adalah makhluk serupa dalam legenda bangsa Athabaskan di Pesisir Pasifik Barat Laut; ia juga kanibal, tetapi dicirikan sebagai tercerahkan dengan wawasan leluhur.",
        "en": "The wechuge is a similar being in the legends of the Athabaskan people of the Northwest Pacific Coast; it too is cannibalistic but is characterized as enlightened with ancestral insights."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c25",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "Sub grouping Algonquian Origin Region Canada United States",
      "locator": "Infobox",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kotak info mencantumkan subkelompok Algonquian dengan wilayah asal Kanada dan Amerika Serikat.",
        "en": "The infobox lists the sub-grouping as Algonquian, with origin region Canada and United States."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c26",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "Comparable forms are in English since 1714 (Whitego).",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk yang sebanding sudah ada dalam bahasa Inggris sejak 1714 (Whitego).",
        "en": "Comparable forms have existed in English since 1714 (Whitego)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c27",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "one of the first appearances of a character inspired by, or named after, a wendigo in non-Indigenous literature is Algernon Blackwood's 1910 novella The Wendigo.",
      "locator": "In popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu kemunculan pertama tokoh yang terinspirasi atau dinamai wendigo dalam sastra non-Pribumi adalah novela The Wendigo karya Algernon Blackwood tahun 1910, yang berbeda dari wujudnya dalam cerita rakyat tradisional.",
        "en": "One of the first appearances of a character inspired by or named after a wendigo in non-Indigenous literature is Algernon Blackwood's 1910 novella The Wendigo, distinct from the wendigo of traditional lore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c28",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "These works set the template for later portrayals in popular culture, at times even replacing the Native American lore.",
      "locator": "In popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karya-karya itu menjadi cetak biru bagi penggambaran budaya populer berikutnya, kadang bahkan menggantikan cerita rakyat Pribumi Amerika.",
        "en": "These works set the template for later portrayals in popular culture, at times even replacing the Native American lore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c29",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "In 1973 a character inspired by the Wendigo appeared in American comic books published by Marvel Comics. ... It first appeared in The Incredible Hulk #162 (April 1973)",
      "locator": "In popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1973 tokoh yang terinspirasi Wendigo muncul dalam komik Amerika terbitan Marvel Comics, pertama kali dalam The Incredible Hulk #162 (April 1973).",
        "en": "In 1973 a character inspired by the Wendigo appeared in American comic books published by Marvel Comics, first in The Incredible Hulk #162 (April 1973)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c30",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "The interactive horror video game Until Dawn (2015) features multiple Wendigos as central antagonists",
      "locator": "In popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gim horor interaktif Until Dawn (2015) menampilkan beberapa Wendigo sebagai antagonis utama.",
        "en": "The interactive horror video game Until Dawn (2015) features multiple Wendigos as central antagonists."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c31",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s1",
      "quote": "the 2021 film Antlers by Scott Cooper, where the wendigo is portrayed as a deer-like creature with a glowing heart",
      "locator": "In popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam film Antlers (2021) karya Scott Cooper, wendigo digambarkan sebagai makhluk mirip rusa dengan jantung bercahaya.",
        "en": "In the 2021 film Antlers by Scott Cooper, the wendigo is portrayed as a deer-like creature with a glowing heart."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c32",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s2",
      "quote": "wiindigoo na windigo: a winter cannibal monster",
      "locator": "Dictionary entry",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Entri kamus mendefinisikan wiindigoo sebagai windigo: monster kanibal musim dingin.",
        "en": "The dictionary entry defines wiindigoo as windigo: a winter cannibal monster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c33",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s3",
      "quote": "The Wendigo is a voracious monster of the First Nations of Canada and Plains Indians culture who appears, under other names, in the stories of many Native American Nations across North America.",
      "locator": "Wendigo",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wendigo adalah monster rakus dari budaya Bangsa-Bangsa Pertama Kanada dan Indian Dataran yang muncul, dengan nama lain, dalam kisah banyak bangsa Pribumi Amerika di seluruh Amerika Utara.",
        "en": "The Wendigo is a voracious monster of the First Nations of Canada and Plains Indians culture who appears, under other names, in the stories of many Native American Nations across North America."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c34",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s3",
      "quote": "According to the Chippewa, the Wendigo was once a human who could never be satisfied and was characterized by excessive selfishness and greed. Such people were thought to eventually become the human-eating monster with a heart of ice who preys upon people.",
      "locator": "Wendigo",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Chippewa, Wendigo dulunya manusia yang tak pernah puas dan bercirikan keegoisan dan keserakahan berlebihan; orang seperti itu dipercaya akhirnya menjadi monster pemakan manusia berhati es yang memangsa orang.",
        "en": "According to the Chippewa, the Wendigo was once a human who could never be satisfied, marked by excessive selfishness and greed; such people were thought to eventually become the human-eating monster with a heart of ice who preys upon people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c35",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s3",
      "quote": "The creature is depicted as an emaciated beast with large horns who is never satiated no matter how much it eats and so is doomed to wander about constantly trying and failing to satisfy its need for comfort.",
      "locator": "Wendigo",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk ini digambarkan sebagai binatang kurus kering bertanduk besar yang tak pernah kenyang berapa pun yang dimakannya, sehingga terus mengembara dan gagal memenuhi kebutuhannya akan kenyamanan.",
        "en": "The creature is depicted as an emaciated beast with large horns that is never satiated no matter how much it eats, and so is doomed to wander constantly, failing to satisfy its need for comfort."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c36",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s3",
      "quote": "The Chippewa origin tale of the monster is echoed by other Nations and serves to reinforce the concept generally held by Native Americans that the needs of the community come before those of the individual.",
      "locator": "Wendigo",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah asal-usul monster dari Chippewa digaungkan bangsa lain dan memperkuat gagasan umum di kalangan Pribumi Amerika bahwa kebutuhan komunitas didahulukan daripada kebutuhan individu.",
        "en": "The Chippewa origin tale of the monster is echoed by other Nations and reinforces the concept generally held by Native Americans that the needs of the community come before those of the individual."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wendigo-c37",
      "source_id": "wendigo-s3",
      "quote": "The Flying Head (or Flying Heads) is similar to the Wendigo in that it is also an insatiable monster that feeds on human flesh. The creature originates in the legends of the Iroquois Nation",
      "locator": "Flying Head",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepala Terbang (Flying Head) mirip Wendigo karena juga monster yang tak pernah puas dan memakan daging manusia, dan berasal dari legenda bangsa Iroquois.",
        "en": "The Flying Head is similar to the Wendigo in that it is also an insatiable monster that feeds on human flesh, and it originates in the legends of the Iroquois Nation."
      }
    }
  ],

      },

        {

            "en": "A giant humanoid with a heart of ice, with a foul stench or chill."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "wendigo-c04"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Anansi atau Ananse adalah tokoh laba-laba dalam agama dan cerita rakyat Akan, penipu yang dikenal mengalahkan lawan yang lebih kuat dengan kecerdikan, dan salah satu tokoh terpenting cerita rakyat Afrika Barat dan Karibia.",
    "en": "Anansi, or Ananse, is the spider character of Akan religion and folklore, a trickster known for outsmarting more powerful opponents with cunning, and one of the most important figures of West African and Caribbean folklore.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "anansi-c05",
      "anansi-c01",
      "anansi-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anansi atau Ananse (secara harfiah \"laba-laba\") adalah tokoh dalam agama dan cerita rakyat Akan yang terkait dengan cerita, kebijaksanaan, pengetahuan, kecerdikan, kenakalan, dan tipu daya, paling sering digambarkan sebagai laba-laba. Berperan sebagai penipu, ia juga salah satu tokoh terpenting dalam cerita rakyat Afrika Barat, Afrika-Amerika, dan Karibia. Ia paling dikenal karena mampu mengalahkan lawan yang lebih kuat lewat kecerdikan, kreativitas, dan kecerdasannya.",
      "en": "Anansi, or Ananse (literally \"spider\"), is a character of Akan religion and folklore tied to stories, wisdom, knowledge, wit, mischief, cunning and trickery, most often shown as a spider. In his trickster role he is also among the most important figures of West African, African-American and Caribbean folklore. He is best known for beating stronger opponents through cunning, creativity and wit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anansi-c05",
        "anansi-c01",
        "anansi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia punya banyak wujud: sering digambarkan sebagai hewan, tetapi juga sebagai laba-laba berwajah manusia atau manusia berciri laba-laba seperti berkaki delapan. Meskipun digambarkan sebagai laki-laki dalam kisahnya, namanya muncul dalam berbagai bentuk, dari Ananse dan Kwaku Ananse hingga bentuk-bentuk Dunia Baru seperti Ba Anansi dan Aunt Nancy. Dalam beberapa kisah ia berkeluarga, dengan istri bernama Okonore Yaa dan beberapa putra.",
      "en": "He has many forms: often shown as an animal, but also as a spider with a human face or a human with spider-like features such as eight legs. Although always male in his stories, his name appears in many forms, from Ananse and Kwaku Ananse to New World versions such as Ba Anansi and Aunt Nancy. In some tales he has a family, with a wife named Okonore Yaa and several sons.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anansi-c04",
        "anansi-c03",
        "anansi-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah kumpulan cerita Gold Coast menceritakan bahwa Laba-laba ingin semua cerita menjadi cerita tentang dirinya, lalu memenuhi tiga syarat Nyankupon dengan tipu daya: lebah dalam guci, ular boa, dan harimau. Versi lain menyebut dewa langit Nyame dengan empat tantangan. Dalam kisah lain, Bapa Anansi menyimpan semua kebijaksanaan dalam guci, tetapi guci itu pecah sehingga kebijaksanaan menyebar ke seluruh dunia.",
      "en": "A collection of Gold Coast tales relates that Spider wanted all stories to be about him and met Nyankupon's three conditions by trickery: bees in a jar, a boa-constrictor and a tiger. Another version names the sky god Nyame and four challenges. In a further tale Father Anansi stored all wisdom in a pot, but the pot broke and wisdom spread throughout the world.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anansi-c21",
        "anansi-c22",
        "anansi-c23",
        "anansi-c24",
        "anansi-c25",
        "anansi-c18",
        "anansi-c26",
        "anansi-c28"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah-kisah itu menyebar ke Amerika melalui perdagangan budak Atlantik. Anansi merupakan bagian dari tradisi yang sepenuhnya lisan, dipandang identik dengan keterampilan berbicara, dan sering dirayakan sebagai lambang perlawanan dan ketahanan budak. Penelitian tentang Jamaika menyebut Anansi sebagai pahlawan budaya Gold Coast, semacam dewa, dan sebagai pahlawan penipu di antara tokoh hewan; di sana kisahnya menjadi hiburan pada malam berjaga. Ia dipandang sekaligus teladan dan kisah peringatan terhadap keinginan egois.",
      "en": "The tales reached the Americas through the Atlantic slave trade. Anansi belonged to a wholly oral tradition, was seen as the embodiment of skilful speech, and was frequently hailed as an emblem of enslaved people's resistance and survival. A study of Jamaica calls Anansi the culture hero of the Gold Coast, a kind of god, and the trickster hero among animal figures; there his tales are entertainment at wake-nights. He is seen as both an ideal and a cautionary tale against selfish desires.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anansi-c08",
        "anansi-c12",
        "anansi-c13",
        "anansi-c34",

        "anansi-c14"
      ]

  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Bagi bangsa Akan, tatanan sosial tersalur lewat cerita; Anansi, tokoh paradoks yang menentang tatanan itu, justru memperkuatnya karena pemberontakan dan keraguan masuk ke dalam iman. Ia juga dipandang sebagai teladan sekaligus kisah peringatan terhadap keinginan egois.",
    "en": "Among the Akan, social order is conveyed through stories; Anansi, a paradoxical figure who defies that order, ends up strengthening it because rebellion and doubt are folded into faith. He is also seen as both an ideal and a cautionary tale against selfish desires.",

      "role": {

        "en": "Trickster who wins the rights to the stories"

      "summary": {
        "id": "Laba-laba ingin semua cerita menjadi tentang dirinya; Nyankupon mensyaratkan guci berisi lebah, ular boa, dan harimau, yang semuanya ia dapatkan dengan tipu daya, sehingga ia boleh menyebut dongeng lama sebagai dongeng Anansi.",
        "en": "Spider wanted all stories to be about him; Nyankupon required a jar of bees, a boa-constrictor and a tiger, all of which he obtained by trickery, so he was allowed to call the old tales Anansi tales."

        "en": "How wisdom became the property of the human race"

        "en": "Keeper of wisdom"
      },
      "summary": {
        "id": "Bapa Anansi menyimpan semua kebijaksanaan dalam sebuah guci untuk menghukum manusia, tetapi putranya Kweku Tsin menyarankan menggantung guci di punggung; dalam amarah ia melempar guci itu hingga pecah dan kebijaksanaan menyebar ke dunia.",
        "en": "Father Anansi stored all wisdom in a pot to punish people, but his son Kweku Tsin suggested hanging it on his back; in anger he threw it down, it broke, and wisdom spread through the world."

      "summary": {

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "anansi-c01",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Taking the role of a trickster, he is also one of the most important characters of West African, African-American and Caribbean folklore.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai penipu, ia juga salah satu tokoh terpenting dalam cerita rakyat Afrika Barat, Afrika-Amerika, dan Karibia.",
        "en": "Taking the role of a trickster, he is also one of the most important characters of West African, African-American and Caribbean folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c02",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi is best known for his ability to outsmart and triumph over more powerful opponents through his use of cunning, creativity and wit.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi dikenal luas atas kemampuannya mengelabui dan mengalahkan lawan tangguh berbekal kecerdikan dan akal muslihat.",
        "en": "Anansi is best known for his ability to outsmart and triumph over more powerful opponents through cunning, creativity and wit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c03",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi is depicted in many different ways and with different names, from \"Ananse\", \"Kwaku Ananse\", and \"Anancy\", to his New World iterations, such as \"Ba Anansi\", \"Kompa Nanzi\" and/or \"Nanzi\", \"Nancy\", \"Aunt Nancy\", and \"Sis' Nancy\", even though he is always depicted as a male in his stories.",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi digambarkan dengan banyak cara dan nama, dari \"Ananse\", \"Kwaku Ananse\", dan \"Anancy\" hingga bentuk Dunia Baru seperti \"Ba Anansi\", \"Kompa Nanzi\", \"Nanzi\", \"Nancy\", \"Aunt Nancy\", dan \"Sis' Nancy\", meskipun dalam kisahnya ia selalu laki-laki.",
        "en": "Anansi is depicted in many ways and under many names, from \"Ananse\", \"Kwaku Ananse\" and \"Anancy\" to New World iterations such as \"Ba Anansi\", \"Kompa Nanzi\", \"Nanzi\", \"Nancy\", \"Aunt Nancy\" and \"Sis' Nancy\", though he is always male in his stories."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c04",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "While often depicted as an animal, Anansi has many representations, which include an anthropomorphic spider with a human face, or conversely, a human with spider-like features, such as eight legs.",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi kerap digambarkan sebagai laba-laba antropomorfik berwajah manusia atau manusia bertubuh laba-laba berkaki delapan.",
        "en": "Anansi has representations including an anthropomorphic spider with a human face or human with spider-like features such as eight legs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c05",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi or Ananse ... literally translates to spider) is a character in Akan religion and folklore associated with stories, wisdom, knowledge, wit, mischievousness, cunning, and trickery, most commonly depicted as a spider.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi atau Ananse (secara harfiah berarti laba-laba) adalah tokoh dalam agama dan cerita rakyat Akan yang terkait dengan cerita, kebijaksanaan, pengetahuan, kecerdikan, kenakalan, dan tipu daya, paling sering digambarkan sebagai laba-laba.",
        "en": "Anansi or Ananse (literally \"spider\") is a character in Akan religion and folklore associated with stories, wisdom, knowledge, wit, mischievousness, cunning and trickery, most commonly depicted as a spider."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c06",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "The Akan people are a close-knit people from present-day southern Ghana who rely on social order, which translates through the stories that come out of their culture.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bangsa Akan adalah masyarakat yang erat dari Ghana selatan masa kini, yang bertumpu pada tatanan sosial yang tersalur lewat cerita-cerita dari budaya mereka.",
        "en": "The Akan are a close-knit people from present-day southern Ghana who rely on social order, which is conveyed through the stories of their culture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c07",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "In many ways, Anansi is a paradoxical character whose actions defy this social order, but in incorporating rebellion and doubt into faith, his folkloric presence strengthens it.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam banyak hal Anansi tokoh paradoks yang tindakannya menentang tatanan sosial, tetapi dengan memasukkan pemberontakan dan keraguan ke dalam iman, kehadirannya dalam cerita rakyat justru memperkuat tatanan itu.",
        "en": "In many ways Anansi is a paradoxical character whose actions defy this social order, but by incorporating rebellion and doubt into faith, his folkloric presence strengthens it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c08",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "These spider tales were spread to the Americas via the Atlantic slave trade.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita-cerita laba-laba ini menyebar ke Amerika melalui perdagangan budak Atlantik.",
        "en": "These spider tales were spread to the Americas via the Atlantic slave trade."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c09",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Spider tales are found extensively throughout West Africa, but the Anansi tales from Ghana are seen to be the origin of these stories and are among the best-known",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita laba-laba ditemukan luas di Afrika Barat, tetapi kisah Anansi dari Ghana dipandang sebagai asal cerita-cerita itu dan termasuk yang paling terkenal.",
        "en": "Spider tales are found extensively throughout West Africa, but the Anansi tales from Ghana are seen as the origin of these stories and are among the best-known."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c10",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "They later spread to the West Indies, Suriname, Sierra Leone (where they were introduced by Jamaican Maroons) and the Netherlands Antilles",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah-kisah itu kemudian menyebar ke Hindia Barat, Suriname, Sierra Leone (dibawa oleh Maroon Jamaika), dan Antilla Belanda.",
        "en": "The tales later spread to the West Indies, Suriname, Sierra Leone (where they were introduced by Jamaican Maroons) and the Netherlands Antilles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c11",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi also has a family in several folktales involving him, consisting of his long-suffering wife Okonore Yaa - known in other regions as Aso, Crooky, or Shi Maria; Ntikuma, his firstborn son; Tikelenkelen, his big-headed son; Nankonhwea, his son with a spindly neck and spindly legs; finally, Afudohwedohwe, his pot-bellied son.",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam beberapa kisah Anansi memiliki keluarga: istrinya yang sabar, Okonore Yaa (di daerah lain dikenal sebagai Aso, Crooky, atau Shi Maria), dan putra-putranya Ntikuma si sulung, Tikelenkelen si berkepala besar, Nankonhwea yang berleher dan berkaki kurus, serta Afudohwedohwe si perut buncit.",
        "en": "In several folktales Anansi has a family: his long-suffering wife Okonore Yaa (known elsewhere as Aso, Crooky or Shi Maria) and his sons Ntikuma the firstborn, Tikelenkelen the big-headed, Nankonhwea with a spindly neck and legs, and Afudohwedohwe the pot-bellied."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c12",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi stories were part of an exclusively oral tradition, and Anansi himself was seen as synonymous with skill and wisdom in speech.",
      "locator": "Oral Tradition",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Anansi merupakan bagian dari tradisi yang sepenuhnya lisan, dan Anansi sendiri dipandang identik dengan keterampilan dan kebijaksanaan berbicara.",
        "en": "Anansi stories were part of an exclusively oral tradition, and Anansi himself was seen as synonymous with skill and wisdom in speech."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c13",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi was often celebrated as a symbol of slave resistance and survival, because Anansi is able to turn the tables on his powerful oppressors by using his cunning and trickery",
      "locator": "Resistance",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi sering dirayakan sebagai lambang perlawanan dan ketahanan budak, karena ia mampu membalikkan keadaan terhadap penindas yang berkuasa lewat kecerdikan dan tipu dayanya.",
        "en": "Anansi was often celebrated as a symbol of slave resistance and survival, because he is able to turn the tables on his powerful oppressors through cunning and trickery."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c14",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi becomes both an ideal to be aspired toward, and a cautionary tale against the selfish desires that can cause our undoing.",
      "locator": "Teaching Narratives",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi menjadi sekaligus teladan yang dicita-citakan dan kisah peringatan terhadap keinginan egois yang dapat menjadi sebab kehancuran kita.",
        "en": "Anansi becomes both an ideal to aspire toward and a cautionary tale against the selfish desires that can cause our undoing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c15",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Kacou Ananzè (Côte d'Ivoire) Kwaku Ananse (Jamaica), (Ghana) Bru Nansi (Virgin Islands) Anansi or Anancy (Jamaica, Grenada, Costa Rica, Colombia, Nicaragua) Anansi (Trinidad and Tobago, Turks and Caicos Islands) Anansi Drew (The Bahamas) Aunt Nancy (South Carolina)",
      "locator": "Other names",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama-nama lain Anansi di berbagai daerah antara lain Kacou Ananzè (Pantai Gading), Kwaku Ananse (Jamaika, Ghana), Bru Nansi (Kepulauan Virgin), Anansi atau Anancy (Jamaika, Grenada, Kosta Rika, Kolombia, Nikaragua), Anansi Drew (Bahama), dan Aunt Nancy (Carolina Selatan).",
        "en": "Names for Anansi in different places include Kacou Ananzè (Côte d'Ivoire), Kwaku Ananse (Jamaica, Ghana), Bru Nansi (Virgin Islands), Anansi or Anancy (Jamaica, Grenada, Costa Rica, Colombia, Nicaragua), Anansi Drew (The Bahamas) and Aunt Nancy (South Carolina)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c16",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi's name comes from the word in the Akan language for \"spider\".",
      "locator": "Origin",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Anansi berasal dari kata dalam bahasa Akan untuk \"laba-laba\".",
        "en": "Anansi's name comes from the word in the Akan language for \"spider\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c17",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "one explains how he became known as the owner of all stories in the world.",
      "locator": "Popular Anansi stories",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu kisah menjelaskan bagaimana ia dikenal sebagai pemilik semua cerita di dunia.",
        "en": "One story explains how he became known as the owner of all stories in the world."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c18",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "Anansi wanted to acquire the stories of the sky god Nyame, who held all the stories to himself. Nyame tasks Anansi with four challenges in exchange for them: the capture of the python Onini, the hornets known as Mmoboro, the leopard Osebo, and the dwarf Mmoatia.",
      "locator": "Akan-Ashanti Anansi stories",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu versi, Anansi ingin mendapatkan cerita milik dewa langit Nyame, yang menyimpan semuanya sendiri; Nyame memberinya empat tantangan sebagai imbalan: menangkap ular piton Onini, tawon Mmoboro, macan tutul Osebo, dan kurcaci Mmoatia.",
        "en": "In one version Anansi wanted to acquire the stories of the sky god Nyame, who held them all to himself; Nyame set him four challenges in exchange: capturing the python Onini, the hornets Mmoboro, the leopard Osebo and the dwarf Mmoatia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c19",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "In the Marvel Comics series The Amazing Spider-Man volume 2 (2003), it is revealed by Ezekiel Sims that Kwaku Anansi was the first Spider-Man.",
      "locator": "References in popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam serial Marvel Comics The Amazing Spider-Man volume 2 (2003), terungkap oleh Ezekiel Sims bahwa Kwaku Anansi adalah Spider-Man pertama.",
        "en": "In the Marvel Comics series The Amazing Spider-Man volume 2 (2003), Ezekiel Sims reveals that Kwaku Anansi was the first Spider-Man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c20",
      "source_id": "anansi-s1",
      "quote": "filmmaker and author Gerald McDermott created the animated short Anansi the Spider in 1969",
      "locator": "References in popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pembuat film dan penulis Gerald McDermott menciptakan film animasi pendek Anansi the Spider pada 1969.",
        "en": "Filmmaker and author Gerald McDermott created the animated short Anansi the Spider in 1969."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c21",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "In the olden days all the stories which men told were stories of Nyankupon, the chief of the gods. Spider, who was very conceited, wanted the stories to be told about him.",
      "locator": "I. How We Got the Name 'Spider Tales'",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada zaman dahulu semua cerita yang dituturkan manusia adalah cerita tentang Nyankupon, pemimpin para dewa; Laba-laba, yang sangat sombong, ingin cerita-cerita itu dituturkan tentang dirinya.",
        "en": "In the olden days all the stories men told were about Nyankupon, the chief of the gods; Spider, who was very conceited, wanted the stories to be told about him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c22",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "Nyankupon agreed, on one condition. He told Spider (or Anansi) that he must bring him three things: the first was a jar full of live bees, the second was a boa-constrictor, and the third a tiger.",
      "locator": "I. How We Got the Name 'Spider Tales'",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyankupon setuju dengan satu syarat: Laba-laba (Anansi) harus membawa tiga hal, yaitu sebuah guci berisi lebah hidup, seekor ular boa, dan seekor harimau.",
        "en": "Nyankupon agreed on one condition: Spider (Anansi) must bring him three things, a jar full of live bees, a boa-constrictor and a tiger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c23",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "The bees immediately declared that of course they could fly into the jar-which they at once did. As soon as they were safely inside, Anansi sealed up the jar and sent it off to Nyankupon.",
      "locator": "I. How We Got the Name 'Spider Tales'",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lebah-lebah langsung menyatakan bahwa tentu mereka bisa terbang masuk ke guci, dan begitu mereka di dalam, Anansi menutup guci itu rapat-rapat lalu mengirimkannya kepada Nyankupon.",
        "en": "The bees declared that of course they could fly into the jar, and once they were safely inside Anansi sealed the jar and sent it to Nyankupon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c24",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "Having thus made the tiger helpless, he led him straight to Nyankupon's house.",
      "locator": "I. How We Got the Name 'Spider Tales'",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah membuat harimau itu tak berdaya, ia menuntunnya langsung ke rumah Nyankupon.",
        "en": "Having made the tiger helpless, he led him straight to Nyankupon's house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c25",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "Nyankupon was amazed at Spider's cleverness in fulfilling the three conditions. He immediately gave him permission for the future to call all the old tales Anansi tales.",
      "locator": "I. How We Got the Name 'Spider Tales'",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyankupon takjub pada kecerdikan Laba-laba memenuhi ketiga syarat itu, dan segera mengizinkannya menyebut semua dongeng lama sebagai dongeng Anansi.",
        "en": "Nyankupon was amazed at Spider's cleverness in fulfilling the three conditions and at once gave him permission to call all the old tales Anansi tales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c26",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "There once lived, in Fanti-land, a man named Father Anansi. He possessed all the wisdom in the world.",
      "locator": "II. How Wisdom Became the Property of the Human Race",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dahulu di tanah Fanti hidup seorang lelaki bernama Bapa Anansi, yang memiliki semua kebijaksanaan di dunia.",
        "en": "Once there lived in Fanti-land a man named Father Anansi, who possessed all the wisdom in the world."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c27",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "One day the men of the country were unfortunate enough to offend Father Anansi, who immediately resolved to punish them. After much thought he decided that the severest penalty he could inflict would be to hide all his wisdom from them.",
      "locator": "II. How Wisdom Became the Property of the Human Race",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suatu hari para lelaki negeri itu menyinggung Bapa Anansi, yang segera bertekad menghukum mereka; setelah berpikir lama ia memutuskan hukuman terberat adalah menyembunyikan seluruh kebijaksanaannya dari mereka.",
        "en": "One day the men of the country offended Father Anansi, who resolved to punish them; after much thought he decided the severest penalty would be to hide all his wisdom from them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c28",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "For some time Kweku Tsin watched his father's vain attempts. At last, unable to contain himself any longer, he cried out: \"Father, why do you not hang the pot on your back? Then you could easily climb the tree.\" ... In his anger he threw the pot down. It struck on a great rock and broke. The wisdom contained in it escaped and spread throughout the world.",
      "locator": "II. How Wisdom Became the Property of the Human Race",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kweku Tsin menyaksikan upaya sia-sia ayahnya dan menyarankan agar guci digantung di punggung; dalam amarah sang ayah melempar guci itu hingga pecah, dan kebijaksanaan di dalamnya lolos serta menyebar ke seluruh dunia.",
        "en": "Kweku Tsin watched his father's vain attempts and suggested hanging the pot on his back; in anger the father threw the pot down so that it broke, and the wisdom in it escaped and spread throughout the world."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c29",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "Anansi persuaded Nothing to change clothes for a little while, promising to give back the fine velvet before they reached the town.",
      "locator": "III. Anansi and Nothing",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi membujuk Nothing bertukar pakaian sebentar dengan janji mengembalikan kain beludru yang bagus sebelum mereka tiba di kota.",
        "en": "Anansi persuaded Nothing to change clothes for a little while, promising to give back the fine velvet before they reached the town."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c30",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "Egya Anansi was very well pleased when he saw his wealth of corn and beans. He was, however, an exceedingly selfish and greedy man, who never liked to share anything",
      "locator": "X. Why Spiders Are Always Found in the Corners of Ceilings",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Egya Anansi digambarkan sebagai lelaki yang sangat egois dan serakah, yang tidak pernah suka berbagi apa pun.",
        "en": "Egya Anansi is described as an exceedingly selfish and greedy man who never liked to share anything."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c31",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "Anansi then became very angry and dealt the figure a blow on the cheek with his right hand. Of course, his hand stuck fast to the rubber.",
      "locator": "X. Why Spiders Are Always Found in the Corners of Ceilings",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi marah dan memukul pipi sosok karet itu dengan tangan kanan, dan tangannya pun lengket pada karet.",
        "en": "Anansi grew angry and struck the figure on the cheek with his right hand, and his hand stuck fast to the rubber."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c32",
      "source_id": "anansi-s2",
      "quote": "He, on the other hand, was so ashamed to be caught in the act of greediness that he changed into a spider and took refuge in a dark corner of the ceiling lest any one should see him. Since then spiders have always been found in dark, dusty corners, where people are not likely to notice them.",
      "locator": "X. Why Spiders Are Always Found in the Corners of Ceilings",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi begitu malu tertangkap basah serakah sehingga ia berubah menjadi laba-laba dan bersembunyi di sudut gelap langit-langit; sejak itu laba-laba selalu ditemukan di sudut gelap dan berdebu.",
        "en": "Anansi was so ashamed to be caught in the act of greediness that he changed into a spider and hid in a dark corner of the ceiling; since then spiders have always been found in dark, dusty corners."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c33",
      "source_id": "anansi-s3",
      "quote": "influences have dominated story-telling in Jamaica ... the second, the conception of the spider Anansi as the trickster hero among a group of animal figures.",
      "locator": "Preface",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu dari dua pengaruh yang mendominasi penceritaan di Jamaika adalah gagasan tentang laba-laba Anansi sebagai pahlawan penipu di antara sekelompok tokoh hewan.",
        "en": "One of two influences dominating story-telling in Jamaica is the conception of the spider Anansi as the trickster hero among a group of animal figures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c34",
      "source_id": "anansi-s3",
      "quote": "Anansi is the culture hero of the Gold Coast,-a kind of god-, just as Turtle is of the Slave coast and Hare (our own Brer Rabbit) of the Bantu people.",
      "locator": "Preface",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anansi adalah pahlawan budaya Gold Coast, semacam dewa, seperti Kura-kura bagi Pantai Budak dan Kelinci (Brer Rabbit) bagi bangsa Bantu.",
        "en": "Anansi is the culture hero of the Gold Coast, a kind of god, just as Turtle is of the Slave coast and Hare (Brer Rabbit) of the Bantu people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c35",
      "source_id": "anansi-s3",
      "quote": "\"Anansi stories\" regularly form the entertainment during wake-nights",
      "locator": "Preface",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita Anansi secara rutin menjadi hiburan pada malam berjaga (wake-night).",
        "en": "\"Anansi stories\" regularly form the entertainment during wake-nights."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anansi-c36",
      "source_id": "anansi-s3",
      "quote": "The head-man on a Westmoreland cattle-pen even assured me that Anansi, once a man, was now leader of the dead",
      "locator": "Preface",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
